\displaystyle\lesssim G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

and

(3.41)

\displaystyle I_{1,2}^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H^{s}}\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H^{s}(\omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Next, we note that there is a natural expansion for
I_{1,1}

I_{1,1}=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\omega A(\mathbf{c})\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\right]\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega,\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}[A(\mathbf{c})\tilde{\mathbf{U}}]\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x.

Applying Proposition
3.1
with
\mathbf{X}=\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}w
, it can be bounded by

\displaystyle I_{1,1}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}\langle A(\mathbf{c})\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x}_{I_{1,1}^{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{2\lambda N^{2}\mu_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}3^{|\tilde{\beta}|}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})|\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\tilde{\beta}\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\,\mathrm{d}x}_{I_{1,1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lambda^{2}N^{2}\mu_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}3^{|\tilde{\beta}|}|\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}=\tilde{\beta}\\
|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{2}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\,\mathrm{d}x}_{I_{1,1}^{3}}.

For the case
\tilde{\beta}=\beta
in
I_{1,1}^{1}
, Proposition
3.1
infers that

(3.42)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\omega\langle A(\mathbf{c})\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{i}\right)^{2}\omega\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{A}\left(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}-\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\right)^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\omega)}^{2}+2\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\omega)}^{2}.

The second part of the right hand side can be bounded as follows, by using the Sobolev embedding
H_{x}^{2}\hookrightarrow L_{x}^{\infty}

and the assumption that
s>3
,

2\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\omega)}^{2}\lesssim\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Again, using Proposition
3.1
, we have

\langle A(\mathbf{c})\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle\leq\|A(\mathbf{c})\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\leq\mu_{A}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|,

which leads to the remaining parts of
I_{1,1}^{1}
being bounded by

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{1}(\tilde{\beta}\neq\beta)

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta\\
\tilde{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega\|\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{A}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}})^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta\\
\tilde{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega)^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2}.

The calculus inequality in
[
48
, Lemma 3.4]
implies

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta\\
\tilde{\beta}\neq\beta\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega)^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\beta}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{x}\omega\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim G_{1}(\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{s})\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}.

The second step applies the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
, assumption
s>3
and the estimate for
\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}
in (
3.22
). Therefore, the following inequality holds

(3.43)

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{1}(\tilde{\beta}\neq\beta)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}})^{2}\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{(s-1)/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Moreover, the integrals
I_{1,1}^{2}
and
I_{1,1}^{3}
can be estimated as

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\lambda N^{2}\mu_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}3^{|\tilde{\beta}|}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})|\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\tilde{\beta}\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda 3^{s}N^{2}\mu_{A}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}})\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}|\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\omega|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{3}=

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}N^{2}\mu_{A}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta}3^{|\tilde{\beta}|}|\partial_{x}^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}\omega|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}=\tilde{\beta}\\
|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{2}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{2}N^{2}3^{s}\mu_{A}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{2}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}|\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\omega|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2},

with the notation
\beta^{\prime}=\beta-\tilde{\beta}
. Since
s>3
, we can use the Sobolev embedding

H_{x}^{s/2}\hookrightarrow L_{x}^{\infty}
, which holds as
s/2>3/2
. In the bound for
I_{1,1}^{2}
, at most one of

|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{3}|
is strictly larger than
|\beta|/2
, leading to the split as follows

\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}=\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{1}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}+\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{3}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}+\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{3}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}.

Each one of them can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{1}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\omega|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{1}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\omega\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta^{\prime}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{3}=\beta\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1\\
|\beta^{\prime}|,|\beta_{1}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\end{subarray}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta^{\prime}}\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s/2}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s/2}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{3}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}.

Therefore, we can deduce the bound for
I_{1,1}^{2}
, using the inequalities (
3.23
) and (
3.39
),

(3.44)

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}})\|\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The estimate for
I_{1,1}^{3}
is similar, leading to

(3.45)

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Controls of integrals II and III

We apply the expressions
\tilde{\bar{u}}=\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}
and

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\frac{\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\mathbf{1}

to the integrals
II
and

, deducing that

(3.46)

II=-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\bar{u}},\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x=-\frac{\alpha}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle=\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{III_{1}}\underbrace{-\frac{\lambda}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}[\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{III_{2}}.

To begin the estimate for the integral
III
, we present the bound for
\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}
,
using the expression (
3.18
) and the estimate on
\|A(\mathbf{c})\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}
in
[

]
,

(3.47)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|A(\mathbf{c})\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\frac{\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\omega\|_{H_{x}^{s}}(1+\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2})\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}+(1+\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}})\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{D}^{1/2}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{D}^{1/2}_{s}(t).

The deduction uses the inequalities (
3.23
), (
3.24
) and (
3.39
). Based on this inequality, we can dominate

and

as

\displaystyle III_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

(3.49)

\displaystyle III_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{\bar{c}_{tot}\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Control of integral IV

Since the expression
N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})=\frac{\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})}-\frac{\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})}
holds, the integral
IV
are divided into the following parts,

\displaystyle IV=

\displaystyle-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}})N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\right)\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{1}}+\underbrace{\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})}\right]\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})}\right]\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{3}}\underbrace{-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\right],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{4}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\right],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{5}}+\underbrace{\frac{\lambda^{3}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\right],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{IV_{6}}.

We first control

(3.50)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

and

\displaystyle IV_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The estimates for

in (

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

\displaystyle+G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

and

\displaystyle IV_{6}\leq

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}^{3/2}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}^{3/2}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Controls of integrals V and VI

Taking the expression
\tilde{\bar{u}}=\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}
into the integral
V
and
using the bound for
\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}
, we obtain

(3.56)

\displaystyle V=-

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\tilde{\bar{u}}),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\frac{\alpha\lambda}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\lambda}{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}\bar{c}_{tot}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}^{1/2}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))\mathscr{E}^{1/2}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The expression
N_{1}(\tilde{\bar{u}})=\frac{\alpha\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})}
implies that the integral
VI
can be expanded as

\displaystyle VI=

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}})N_{1}(\bar{u})],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\alpha\lambda}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right]\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{VI_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{\alpha\lambda^{2}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\right],\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{VI_{2}}.

Estimate for

is derived by using the inequality (

),

(3.57)

\displaystyle VI_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\lambda}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\lesssim G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}^{1/2}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{1}G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1/2-\gamma/4}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Finally, by summing all inequalities above for
|\beta|\leq s
,
we complete the estimate for
\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}
,
that is

(3.59)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}+\left(\lambda(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}-\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right)\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}^{2}+\left(\frac{\alpha}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}}{4\eta_{1}}-\frac{\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}}{4\eta_{2}}\right)\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The
H^{s}
estimates for
\tilde{T}
and
\tilde{c}_{tot}

The evolution equation for
\tilde{T}
is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{T}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}(1+\lambda\tilde{T})\right]-\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}}(1+\lambda\tilde{T})-\alpha\lambda\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}=0.

By taking derivative
\partial_{x}^{\beta}
of this equation with
|\beta|\leq s
, multiplying by
\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}
and
integrating with respect to
x
over
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, we deduce that

(3.60)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\frac{2\alpha}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1}}\underbrace{-\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{T}\right),\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}}\left(1+\lambda\tilde{T}\right)\right],\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{3}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\alpha\lambda\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}\right),\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{4}}.

Before dominating these integrals, we rewrite the relation (
3.19
)

\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}=-\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}}{1+\lambda\tilde{T}}.

Using it to replace
\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}
, the integral
VII_{1}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle VII_{1}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{2\alpha}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right),\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right)\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1,1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right),\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{T}\right)\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1,2}}.

Applying the inequalities (
3.24
) and (
3.35
), we control the second term as

(3.61)

VII_{1,2}\lesssim\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\lesssim G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The integral
VII_{1,1}
needs a more refined estimate. Due to the decomposition

\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}=\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})},

this integral can be divided into the following parts

\displaystyle VII_{1,1}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{2\alpha}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1,1}^{1}}\underbrace{-\frac{4\alpha\lambda}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot},\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right)\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1,1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{2\alpha\lambda^{2}}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right),\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\right)\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}}_{VII_{1,1}^{3}}.

We control them separately, writing as follows

(3.62)

VII_{1,1}^{1}=\frac{2\alpha}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2},

(3.63)

\displaystyle VII_{1,1}^{2}\lesssim\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim G_{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

and

(3.64)

\displaystyle VII_{1,1}^{3}\lesssim\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

For the integrals
VII_{2}
and
VII_{4}
, we again replace
\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}
with the relation (
3.19
) and apply the inequality (
3.35
), deducing that

(3.65)

\displaystyle VII_{2}=\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\tilde{T}\right),\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}(1+\lambda\tilde{T})\right]\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(1+\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}})\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t),

(3.66)

\displaystyle VII_{4}=

\displaystyle-\alpha\lambda\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}}(1+\lambda\tilde{T})\right],\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}})\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The calculations for
VII_{3}
is same to that for
VII_{4}
,

(3.67)

\displaystyle VII_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\langle\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left[\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}}\left(1+\lambda\tilde{T}\right)\right],\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{T}\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G_{2}^{2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

Summing all above inequalities for
|\beta|\leq s
, now we establish the
H_{x}^{s}
norm estimate for
\tilde{T}
, i.e.

(3.68)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}-\frac{2\alpha}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\lesssim(G_{2}+G_{2}^{2})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+1/2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

At last, the mass conservation equations (
3.13
) have a derivation for
\tilde{c}_{tot}
, i.e.

\partial_{t}\tilde{c}_{tot}=\alpha\Delta_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}.

Applying derivative
\partial_{x}^{\beta}
to this equation (
|\beta|\leq s
), multiplying by
\chi\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}

and integrating with respect to
x
over
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, we obtain the equality

\frac{\chi}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\alpha\chi\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}=0,

where
\chi
is defined in (
3.26
). It is clear that the estimate for
\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}

can be expressed as

(3.69)

\frac{\chi}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}+\alpha\chi\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}=0.

The energy estimate for the perturbations

Now we sum the inequalities (
3.59
), (
3.68
) and (
3.69
), obtaining

(3.70)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathscr{E}_{s}

\displaystyle(t)+\left(\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}-\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right)\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega)}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle+\left(\alpha\chi+\frac{\alpha}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{2\alpha}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}-\frac{\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)}{4\eta_{1}}-\frac{\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)}{4\eta_{2}}\right)\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}(1+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}_{s}(t).

The constant
C_{s}>0
depends on
s,N,\mu_{A},\lambda_{A},\alpha,\bar{\mathbf{c}},C_{Sob},|\mathbb{T}^{3}|
.
The bound for
\omega^{-1}
uses the Sobolev embedding, that is

\|\omega^{-1}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}=\|(1+\lambda\tilde{T})^{1-\gamma/2}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq(1+\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}})^{1-\gamma/2}\leq(1+C_{Sob}\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}})^{1-\gamma/2}.

Let the constant
\eta_{1}=\eta_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}}{3}
, the definitions of
d_{1}
and
\chi
deduce that we recover inequality (
3.31
).

Since the functionals
G_{1}=G_{1}(I_{1})
and
G_{2}=G_{2}(I_{2})
depend on the composite functions
H_{1}(\lambda\tilde{T})
and

H_{2}(\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})
, there exists a positive constant
\gamma_{1}
such that

(3.71)

G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2}|_{I_{1}=I_{2}=[-\frac{1}{2},+\frac{1}{2}]}\leq\gamma_{1}.

This is caused by the smoothness and continuity of the functions
H_{1},H_{2}
and
\tilde{T},\tilde{c}_{tot}
. Moreover, we can find a positive
constant
\gamma_{0}<\gamma_{1}
such that

(3.72)

G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2}|_{I_{1}=I_{2}=[-\frac{1}{4},+\frac{1}{4}]}\leq\gamma_{0},

and there exists a constant
0<\iota_{0}<1
such that
\gamma_{0}\leq\iota_{0}\gamma_{1}<\gamma_{1}
. Recall that we choose

I_{1}=[-\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},+\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}]
and

I_{2}=[-\|\tilde{c}_{tot},+\|\tilde{c}_{tot}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}]
to ensure the inequalities
(
3.23
) and (
3.24
) hold in the previous discussion. Using the Sobolev embedding, we can deduce the following bounds from the assumptions on the initial data in Proposition
3.1
,

\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{in}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},\|\tilde{T}^{in}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq\max\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\chi}}\}C_{Sob}\delta_{MS}\leq 1/2,

\text{ and}\quad C_{s}(1+\gamma_{1})(1+\delta_{MS})^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\delta_{MS}+\delta_{MS}^{2s+4})\delta_{MS}\leq 1/2.

Obviously, the initial data satisfy
I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq[-\frac{1}{2},+\frac{1}{2}]
. Thus, for the initial time, the functionals can be bounded by

G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{1}.

Due to the definitions of the constants
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
, the inequality (
3.31
) becomes

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)+\mathscr{D}_{s}(t)\leq 0,

for time
t=0
. We introduce

\displaystyle T^{\ast}:=\sup

\displaystyle\left\{\tau>0;\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\max\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\chi}}\}C_{Sob}\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{2\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\text{ and }C_{s}(1+\gamma_{1})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq\frac{1}{2}\right\},

constant
C_{s}
coincides with that in (
3.31
). Then the above inequality holds on
t\in[0,T^{\ast}]
. We then prove
T^{\ast}=+\infty
. Otherwise, if
T^{\ast}<+\infty
, it is clear that for all
t\in[0,T^{\ast}]
, we have

\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{D}_{s}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq\mathscr{E}_{s}(0)\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}.

The assumptions on
\delta_{MS}
(
3.32
) imply that

\max\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\chi}}\}C_{Sob}\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{4\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, the above discussions on functionals
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
deduce that for any
t\in[0,T^{\ast}]
,

1+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{0}\leq\iota_{0}\gamma_{1}<\gamma_{1}.

It guarantees that the following inequality holds for any
t\in[0,T^{\ast}]

\displaystyle C_{s}(1+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}(1+\iota\gamma_{1})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)

\displaystyle<C_{s}(1+\gamma_{1})(1+\delta_{MS})^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\delta_{MS}+\delta_{MS}^{2s+4})\delta_{MS}\leq\frac{1}{4}<\frac{1}{2}.

Due to the continuity of the energy functional
\mathscr{E}_{s}(t)
, there exists a small constant
\tau_{0}>0
such that
for all
t\in[0,T^{\ast}+\tau_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\max\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\chi}}\}C_{Sob}\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{2\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}

\displaystyle C_{s}(1+\gamma_{1})(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t))^{1-\gamma/2}(1+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)+\mathscr{E}_{s}^{s+2}(t))\mathscr{E}_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq\frac{1}{2},

contradicting the definition of
T^{\ast}
. Therefore,
T^{\ast}=+\infty
. We conclude that the inequality (
3.33
) holds for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Moreover, we prove the positivities of the concentrations and the temperature. Applying the Sobolev embedding and the assumption (
3.32
) for the bound
\delta_{MS}
, we have, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}

\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)\leq C_{Sob}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}(t)\leq C_{Sob}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}(t)\leq C_{Sob}(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})\delta_{MS}\leq\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{2},

\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)\leq C_{Sob}\|\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}(t)\leq C_{Sob}\delta_{MS}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Thus the positivities hold as

\bar{c}_{i}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{i}(t,x)\geq\bar{c}_{i}-\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)\geq\frac{\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}}{2}>0,\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq N,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3},\lambda\in(0,1]

and

1+\lambda\tilde{T}(t,x)\geq 1-\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}>0\quad\forall(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3},\lambda\in(0,1].

In addition, the flux-force relations (
3.14
) and Proposition
3.1
imply that there exists a positive constant
C_{MS}
, such that the following inequality holds for any
t\geq 0
,

(3.73)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}(t)=

\displaystyle\|(1+\lambda\tilde{T})^{1-\gamma/2}A(\mathbf{c})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}+(1+\lambda\tilde{T})^{-\gamma/2}\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}A(\mathbf{c})^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}})\|_{H_{x}^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\bar{\mathbf{c}},s,N,\delta_{MS},\lambda_{A},\mu_{A},C_{Sob},|\mathbb{T}^{3}|)(\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{MS}\delta_{MS}.

The constant
C_{MS}
is independent of the parameters
\lambda
and
\delta_{MS}
, since we impose an upper bound for
\delta_{MS}
in the assumption.
∎

3.2.
Existence and Uniqueness of the Solution to the Maxwell-Stefan System

Existence of the perturbation solution

We first emphasize that the results in Proposition
3.1
can be directly applied, since the assumptions there
are the same as those in Theorem
3.1
. We will develop a so-called
pseudo-nonlinear iterative approximated method
on the time interval
[0,T_{0}]

with any positive constant
T_{0}
, and initially set

\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(0)}:=\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{in},\quad\tilde{T}^{(0)}:=\tilde{T}^{in}.

The assumptions on the initial data ensure that

\mathscr{E}_{s}^{(0)}:=\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(0)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}+\|\tilde{T}^{(0)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}+\chi\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{c}_{i}^{(0)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}.

The assumptions (A2) and (A3
′
) infer that we can define
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(0)}:=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{in}
, which satisfies
not only
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(0)}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
but also the flux-force relations. We assume that
there are
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
and
\tilde{T}^{(n)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
with
n\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, satisfying that
for any
(t,x)\in[0,T_{0}]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}

(3.74)

(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n-1)})\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n)}+(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n-1)})\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}=0,\quad\text{with }\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{c}_{i}^{(n)},

and for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]

\mathscr{E}_{s}^{(n)}(t):=\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}(t)+\|\tilde{T}^{(n)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t)+\chi\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}(t)\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}.

Define
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n)}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n-1)})^{1-\gamma/2}A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n-1)})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle+(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n-1)})^{-\gamma/2}A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n-1)})^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n-1))})\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n)},

then
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n)}
belongs to
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s-1})
.
By induction, we define
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)},\tilde{T}^{(n+1)})
as

(3.75)

\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)})+\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)})=0,\quad\text{with }\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{c}_{i}^{(n+1)},

which enables us to define
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
as

(3.76)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}:=

\displaystyle(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)})^{1-\gamma/2}A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})^{-1}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle+(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)})^{-\gamma/2}A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n))})\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}.

The quantities
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}
and
\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
satisfy the following equations

(3.77)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}-\frac{\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}}\mathbf{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}}\mathbf{1}\right]=0,

(3.78)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[\frac{1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\frac{1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)})^{2}}|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle-\alpha\lambda\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}}=0,

supplemented with the initial data that for almost any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}

(3.79)

\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}(0,x)=\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{in}(x),\quad\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}(0,x)=\tilde{T}^{in}(x).

The equation (
3.77
) for
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}
can also be written in a form similar to the equation
(
3.13
). It has a natural derivation by taking the scalar product with
\mathbf{1}
in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

(3.80)

\partial_{t}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}=\alpha\Delta_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}.

The main features of the pseudo-nonlinear iterative approximated method are as follows: Although the equation (
3.78
) appears nonlinear due to the terms
|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}|^{2}
and
\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
,
we clarify that it is actually linear with respect to
\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
.
This is because
\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}
is solvable due to the heat equation (
3.80
).

Now we introduce the functionals

(3.81)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{s}^{(n+1)}(t):=\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}(t)+\|\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t)+\chi\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}},

(3.82)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{s}^{(n+1)}(t):=d_{1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega^{(n)})}^{2}(t)+d_{2}\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t),

with the notation
\omega^{(n)}:=(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)})^{\gamma/2-1}
. Following the above calculations in the energy estimate, we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{(n+1)}_{s}(t)+d_{1}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\omega^{(n)})}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha}{\bar{c}_{tot}}+\frac{2\alpha}{3\bar{c}_{tot}^{2}}+\frac{6(\frac{3}{2})^{1-\frac{\gamma}{2}}-3[1+C_{Sob}(\mathscr{E}^{(n)}_{s})^{1/2}(t)]^{1-\frac{\gamma}{2}}}{2\lambda_{A}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}}\right)\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}(1+G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{2}^{2})[1+(\mathscr{E}^{(n)}_{s})^{1/2}(t)]^{1-\gamma/2}

\displaystyle\times[1+(\mathscr{E}^{(n)}_{s})^{1/2}(t)+(\mathscr{E}^{(n)}_{s})^{s+2}(t)](\mathscr{E}^{(n)}_{s})^{1/2}(t)\mathscr{D}^{(n+1)}_{s}(t).

The constant
C_{s}
coincides with the one in inequality (
3.31
). Here the functionals
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
depend on the intervals
I_{1}=[-\|\tilde{T}^{(n)}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},+\|\tilde{T}^{(n)}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}]
and

I_{2}=[-\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},+\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}]
.
Since
\mathscr{E}_{s}^{(n)}(t)\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}
for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
, by repeating the previous discussions in the energy estimate, we finally can deduce that for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]

(3.83)

\mathscr{E}^{(n+1)}_{s}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{D}^{(n+1)}_{s}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq\mathscr{E}^{(n+1)}_{s}(0)\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}.

Based on this inequality, there exist
\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}
to the equation (
3.80
) that
belongs to
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
and

\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}
that belongs to
L^{2}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
. When
\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}
is known and
the relation (
3.75
) holds, the equation (
3.78
) is linear to
\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
.
It is not hard to deduce that there exists
\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
belonging to
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
for this equation.
Then (
3.77
) is a linear hyperbolic-parabolic equation for
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}
,
standard method raises the existence of
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)}
belonging to
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
.
Moreover, the relation
\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{c}_{i}^{(n+1)}
holds
since the solution to (
3.80
) is unique.
At last, we derive
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s-1})
from its definition,
and
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n+1)},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(n+1)},\tilde{T}^{(n+1)})
satisfies the inequality (
3.83
).

By induction, there exist sequences
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\tilde{T}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, having a uniform bound. We rewrite the equation for
\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}
as

(3.84)

\partial_{t}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}+\frac{2\alpha}{3}\Delta_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}+\frac{\alpha\lambda}{3}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}|^{2}}{1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)}}=0,

by applying the relation (
3.75
) in the form

\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n+1)}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{(n)}}=-\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}}{1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)}}
. Due to the Sobolev embedding
H_{x}^{2}\hookrightarrow L_{x}^{\infty}
and the assumption
s>3
,
it is not hard to find a polynomial
\mathcal{P}_{1}
, with coefficients depending on
\alpha,s
,
such that for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\|\tilde{T}^{(n)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}},\|\tilde{T}^{(n+1)}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\right)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{1}(\delta_{MS}).

Using the Aubin-Lions Theorem, it deduces that there exists
\tilde{T}^{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})

such that for any
s^{\prime}<s
, up to a subsequence,

•

(\tilde{T}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\tilde{T}^{\infty}
weakly in

L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
(specifically, weakly-
\ast
in
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0})
),

•

(\tilde{T}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\tilde{T}^{\infty}

strongly in
C^{0}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s^{\prime}})
.

Integrating (
3.84
) against a test function, the nonlinear terms in it can be bounded
and dealt with by applying the strong convergence in
H_{x}^{s^{\prime}}
. Therefore, it can pass to the limit as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, writing as

(3.85)

\partial_{t}\tilde{T}^{\infty}+\frac{2\alpha}{3}\Delta_{x}\tilde{T}^{\infty}+\frac{\alpha\lambda}{3}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{\infty}|^{2}}{1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{\infty}}=0.

Similar discussions can be applied on the sequence
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, deducing that there exists
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
, such that for any
s^{\prime}<s
, up to a subsequence,

•

(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty}

weakly in
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s})
(specifically, weakly-
\ast
in
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0})
),

•

(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty}

strongly in
C^{0}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s^{\prime}})
.

Define
\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{c}_{i}^{\infty}
, it is not hard to notice that the equation (
3.75
) can also pass to
the limit as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, leading to

\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{\infty})+\nabla_{x}\tilde{T}^{\infty}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty})=0.

Then we can define
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
as

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}=(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{\infty})^{1-\gamma/2}[A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty})]^{-1}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}+(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{\infty})^{-\gamma/2}[A(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty})]^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty})\nabla_{x}\tilde{T},

which belongs to
L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s-1})
. Since we can deal with the nonlinear terms by applying the strong convergences, the evolution equations for

(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
can also pass to the limit

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty}+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty})\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}-\frac{\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}}\mathbf{1}\right)\right]-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty})\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}}\mathbf{1}\right]=0.

Here we use the fact that
(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{(n)})^{\gamma/2}
converges strongly to
(1+\lambda\tilde{T}^{\infty})^{\gamma/2}

in
H_{x}^{s^{\prime}}
, obtained from the Dominated Convergence Theorem. We conclude that

(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty},\tilde{T}^{\infty})
is a classical solution to the system of equations (
3.13
)-(
3.14
)-(
3.15
). Moreover, it is not hard to check

\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};L_{x}^{2})
, resulting in

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty}\in C^{0}(0,T_{0};H_{x}^{s^{\prime}-1})
.
Finally, by using the continuity of
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\infty},\tilde{T}^{\infty})
, we conclude that the following
inequality holds for
t\in[0,T_{0}]

\mathscr{E}_{s}^{\infty}(t):=\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}^{\infty}\|_{H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}(t)+\|\tilde{T}^{\infty}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}(t)+\chi\|\tilde{c}_{tot}^{\infty}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\leq\delta_{MS}^{2},

owing to the energy estimates established in the previous.

Reset
t=T_{0}
as an initial moment and repeat the above processes on the time interval

[T_{0},2T_{0}]
, the corresponding energy functional can be bounded as
\mathscr{E}_{s}^{\infty}(2T_{0})\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}
.
By induction, we conclude that there is a classical solution for the perturbative system
(
3.13
)-(
3.14
)-(
3.15
) on
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Uniqueness of the perturbation solution

Consider
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{T})
and
(\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{V}},\tilde{\theta})
are two solutions to the system of equations (
3.13
)-(
3.14
)-(
3.15
) and satisfy the assumptions in Theorem
3.1
. We introduce the notations frequently used below

\mathbf{d}=\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\quad\tilde{d}_{tot}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{d}_{i},\quad\tilde{\bar{v}}=\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}},\quad M_{1}(\tilde{\bar{v}})=\frac{\tilde{d}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}\tilde{\bar{v}},

\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\ast}=\tilde{\mathbf{V}}-\frac{\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\mathbf{1},\quad M_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})=\frac{\langle\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\tilde{d}_{tot}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})},

and denote
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}=\tilde{\mathbf{c}}-\tilde{\mathbf{d}}
,
\tilde{\mathbf{W}}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}
with
\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
and

\tilde{I}=\tilde{T}-\tilde{\theta}
. The corresponding notations are
\tilde{h}_{tot}=\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{1}\rangle
and

\tilde{\mathbf{W}}^{\ast}=\tilde{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}\mathbf{1}
,

\tilde{\bar{w}}=\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}
.
Recalling the equation (
3.17
) that
\tilde{c}_{tot}
and
\tilde{d}_{tot}
satisfy, the equation for
\tilde{h}_{tot}
is

\partial_{t}\tilde{h}_{tot}=\alpha\Delta_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot},\quad\alpha>0,

with the initial data
\tilde{h}_{tot}(0,x)=0
a.e.
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
. It is clear that
\tilde{h}_{tot}=0
for a.e.
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
Next, the subtraction of the evolution equations for temperatures is

(3.86)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{I}-\frac{2\alpha\lambda}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left[\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}\tilde{I}\right]-\frac{2\alpha\lambda^{2}}{3}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})^{2}}\tilde{I}-\alpha\lambda\frac{\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{I}=0.

It uses the following calculations

\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}-\frac{1}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}=\frac{-\lambda\tilde{h}_{tot}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})}=0,

\frac{1}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}}-\frac{1}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})^{2}}=\frac{-2\lambda\bar{c}_{tot}\tilde{h}_{tot}-\lambda^{2}(\tilde{c}_{tot}+\tilde{d}_{tot})\tilde{h}_{tot}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})^{2}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})^{2}}=0,

and

|\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}-|\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}|^{2}=\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot}+\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\cdot\tilde{h}_{tot}=0,

which hold since
\tilde{h}_{tot}=0
. Moreover, the solvability conditions for
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}
provide relations

\nabla_{x}\tilde{I}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})+\lambda\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{I}=0,

and

\nabla_{x}\tilde{I}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})+\lambda\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{I}=0.

The difference is caused by the equality

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{T}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}=\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{T}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{T}+\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{T}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}=\nabla_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot}\tilde{T}+\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{I}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{T}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}+\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}=\nabla_{x}\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{I}+\nabla_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot}\tilde{\theta}.

We further deduce the expression

\nabla_{x}\tilde{I}=-\frac{\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}\tilde{I}=-\frac{\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}}\tilde{I}.

Now combining the relation
|\tilde{c}_{tot}|^{2}=|\tilde{d}_{tot}|^{2}
, we can obtain the
L_{x}^{2}
norm of
\tilde{I}
:

(3.87)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{I}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}=\frac{\alpha\lambda^{2}}{3}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\tilde{d}_{tot}|^{2}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})^{2}}\tilde{I}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\delta_{MS}^{2}\|\tilde{I}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2},

where we use the assumptions that
\|\tilde{d}_{tot}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}^{2}\leq\delta_{MS}^{2}
with
s>3
and the Sobolev embedding. We conclude that
\tilde{I}=0

almost everywhere in
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
, deduced from Grönwall’s inequality given the initial data
\tilde{I}^{in}=0
.

Finally, we focus on the subtraction of the mass conservation equations and the flux-force relations, writing as

(3.88)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}+\bar{\mathbf{c}}(\nabla_{x}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{W}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+\lambda\nabla_{x}\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast})+\lambda\nabla_{x}\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}^{\ast})-\lambda\nabla_{x}\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\tilde{\bar{u}})-\lambda\nabla_{x}\cdot[\bar{\mathbf{c}}(N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-M_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}))]

\displaystyle-\lambda^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}))-\lambda^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot[\tilde{\mathbf{d}}(N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-M_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}))]+\lambda^{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\tilde{\mathbf{h}}N_{1}(\tilde{\bar{u}}))=0,

(3.89)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\theta}=(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})^{\gamma/2}[A(\mathbf{c})-A(\mathbf{d})]\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})^{\gamma/2}A(\mathbf{d})\tilde{\mathbf{W}}.

The deduction uses the fact that
\tilde{h}_{tot}=0
,
\tilde{I}=0

\tilde{T}=\tilde{\theta}
,

\tilde{\bar{w}}=\frac{\alpha\nabla_{x}\tilde{h}_{tot}}{\bar{c}_{tot}}=0
, and

N_{1}(\tilde{\bar{u}})-M_{1}(\tilde{\bar{v}})=\frac{\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{\bar{w}}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}\tilde{h}_{tot}\tilde{\bar{v}}}{(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot})(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})}+\frac{\tilde{h}_{tot}\tilde{\bar{v}}}{\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{c}_{tot}}=0.

By applying the expression

(3.90)

N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-M_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}})=\frac{\langle\mathbf{h},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\tilde{d}_{tot}(\lambda\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle+\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle)}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})},

we obtain the
L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})
norm of
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{W}},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x+\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}^{\ast},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\tilde{\bar{u}},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}}N_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}),\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\left(\frac{\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})}\right),\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{d}}\left(\frac{\langle\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}}-\frac{\tilde{d}_{tot}\langle\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\lambda\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle}{\bar{c}_{tot}(\bar{c}_{tot}+\lambda\tilde{d}_{tot})}\right),\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{h}}N_{1}(\tilde{\bar{u}}),\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\rangle_{\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Replacing
\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
as we have done in the energy estimate, the first integral on the right hand side can be bounded as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{W}},\nabla_{x}\mathbf{h}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{W}},(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})^{\gamma/2-1}A(\mathbf{d})\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{W}},(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})^{\gamma/2-1}[A(\mathbf{c})-A(\mathbf{d})]\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\theta}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\lambda_{A}}{2}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}(\delta_{MS})\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}},

where
\mathcal{P}(\delta_{MS})>0
is a polynomial in
\delta_{MS}
whose coefficients only depend on

\bar{\mathbf{c}}
and
N
. This inequality also uses the expression of the MS matrix
A
and the estimate

|c_{i}c_{j}-d_{i}d_{j}|=|c_{i}c_{j}-c_{i}d_{j}+c_{i}d_{j}-d_{i}d_{j}|=\lambda|c_{i}\tilde{h}_{j}+\tilde{h}_{i}d_{j}|\leq\lambda C(|\tilde{h}_{i}|+|\tilde{h}_{j}|).

After calculations, we finally deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda_{A}}{2}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})(\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\lambda_{A}}{2}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}+2\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})\right)\|\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}.

Here we also replace
\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}
using the equation (
3.89
).
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})>0
denotes another polynomial in
\delta_{MS}
. Choosing
\delta_{MS}
small enough so that

-\frac{\lambda_{A}}{2}(\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})^{2}+2\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})\leq 0
, we deduce that

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}^{2}\leq 2(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i})\delta_{MS}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\delta_{MS})\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}.

Grönwall’s inequality implies that
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}(t,x)=0
for almost any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
, due to
the initial data
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}(0,x)=0
for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
. It means
\tilde{\mathbf{c}}=\tilde{\mathbf{d}}
, then the
equation (
3.89
) becomes

(1+\lambda\tilde{\theta})^{\gamma/2}A(\mathbf{d})\tilde{\mathbf{W}}=0,

inferring that
\tilde{\mathbf{W}}=0
, as
1+\lambda\tilde{\theta}(t,x)>0
for almost any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{W}}\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
. We conclude that the solution
(\tilde{\mathbf{c}},\tilde{\mathbf{U}},\tilde{T})
is equal to

(\tilde{\mathbf{d}},\tilde{\mathbf{V}},\tilde{\theta})
and the uniqueness is established.

4.
The Diffusion Asymptotics Process

We again emphasize that we only consider the Boltzmann equations with collision kernels satisfying assumptions (H1)-(H2)-(H3)-(H4)
with
\gamma\in[0,1]
(i.e. the hard potential and the Maxwellian potential cases) in this Section,
since we need the explicit spectral gap for the operator

\mathbf{L}

[
16
,
22
]
.

In the first part, we will establish the hypocoercive properties satisfied by the Boltzmann operators
\mathbf{L}^{\varepsilon}
and

\mathbf{\Gamma}
, some estimates for the source term
\mathbf{S}^{\varepsilon}
following methods in
[
6
]
, and a crucial Poincaré inequality for
\mathbf{f}
, as preparations. The proof for the perturbative Cauchy theory of the Boltzmann multi-species equations states in the second part.

4.1.
Preparations for A Priori Estimate of the Perturbation
\mathbf{f}

We consider the local Maxwellian
\mathbf{M}^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
2.11
), whose macroscopic quantities are the unique solution in Theorem
2.1
in the form (
2.12
). These macroscopic quantities have the following properties and estimates

\displaystyle\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\bar{c}_{i}+\varepsilon\tilde{c}_{i}(t,x)>0,\quad 1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}(t,x)>0,\quad\text{a.e. }(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{c}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{s}(\bar{\mathbf{c}}^{-1})}\leq\delta_{MS},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{s}}\leq\delta_{MS}.

Moreover, we can easily find a positive constant
C_{MS}
from the decomposition (
3.4
), such that

\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{s-1}}\leq C_{MS}\delta_{MS}.

The constant
C_{MS}
is independent of
\varepsilon
and
\delta_{MS}
. Computation details for its
\mathrm{Span}(\mathbf{1})^{\perp}
part lie in (
3.73
)

We first present some estimates for the local Maxwellian
\mathbf{M}^{\varepsilon}
.
Following the notations in
[
6
]
, we define the local Maxwellian

\bm{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{1},\varepsilon\mathbf{u},T\mathbf{1})
and the global Maxwellian

\bm{\mathcal{M}}=\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{1},0,\mathbf{1})
. Then we have the expressions

\mathbf{M}^{\varepsilon}=(\bar{\mathbf{c}}+\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{c}})\bm{\mathcal{M}}
and

\bm{\mu}=\bar{\mathbf{c}}\bm{\mathcal{M}}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Consider the local Maxwellian
\mathbf{M}^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
2.11
), where

(\mathbf{c},\mathbf{u},T)
is the unique perturbation solution
stated in Theorem
2.1
. Then for any

\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
1\leq i\leq N
, when parameter

(4.1)

\delta\in(0,1-\delta_{MS}),

there exist explicit constants
C_{\delta}^{low}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{low}(\delta)
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that for any

(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

(4.2)

M_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\geq C_{\delta}^{low}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{low}(\delta)c_{i}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{1/\delta}.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
1\leq i\leq N
, when parameter

(4.3)

\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{1+\delta_{MS}}),

there exist explicit constants
C_{\delta}^{up}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{up}(\delta)
, such that for any

(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

(4.4)

M_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\leq C_{\delta}^{up}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{up}(\delta)c_{i}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta}.

Moreover, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
1\leq i\leq N
, when
\delta
ranges in (
4.3
), there
exists a constant
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{sub}(\delta)
, such that we can bound the following subtraction, for any

(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

(4.5)

|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{M}_{i}|(t,x,v)\leq C_{\delta}^{up}(\varepsilon|u_{i}|+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|)\mathcal{R}_{i}^{sub}(\delta)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta}.

Proof.

For any
\delta>0
and for any
1\leq i\leq N
, we first establish the lower bound for
M_{i}^{\varepsilon}=c_{i}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}
,
writing
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}
in the form

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)=\mathcal{M}_{i}^{1/\delta}(v)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-1/\delta}(v),\quad t\geq 0,x\in\mathbb{T}^{3},v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

The product is expressed as

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-1/\delta}=\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(\delta-1)}{2\delta}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{2}(\frac{1}{\delta}-\frac{1}{T})+\frac{\varepsilon m_{i}v\cdot u_{i}}{T}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2T}\right\},

where
T=1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}>0
, since
\tilde{T}
is small enough. To find a uniform lower bound for this product
with respect to
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we choose

(4.6)

\delta\in(0,1-\delta_{MS}),

which ensures that
\frac{1}{\delta}-\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}>0
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
.
Then we can obtain

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{\delta}}\geq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(\delta-1)}{2\delta}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{2}(\frac{1}{\delta}-\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|})-\frac{\varepsilon m_{i}|u_{i}||v|}{1-\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|)}\right\},

where the index of the exponent on the right hand side is a quadratic function with respect to
|v|
. Its minimum value occurs at

|v|=\frac{\delta\varepsilon|u_{i}|}{(1-\delta)-\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|}
, that is, for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-1/\delta}(v)\geq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(\delta-1)}{2\delta}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-\delta)-2\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|}\right\}.

The lower bound for
M_{i}^{\varepsilon}
is, for any
1\leq i\leq N

M_{i}^{\varepsilon}(v)\geq C_{\delta}^{low}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{low}(\delta)c_{i}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{1/\delta},\quad\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

with notations

C_{\delta}^{low}=\min_{1\leq i\leq N}\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(\delta-1)}{2\delta}},\quad\mathcal{R}_{i}^{low}(\delta)=\left(\frac{1}{1+|\tilde{T}|}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|-\delta)}\right\}.

Next, the upper bound for
M_{i}^{\varepsilon}
(
1\leq i\leq N
) uses similar calculations. For any
\delta>0
, the local Maxwellian
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}
can be expressed as

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)=\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta}(v)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}(v),

where the product has the bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)|v|^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon m_{i}u_{i}\cdot v}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1+\varepsilon\tilde{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)|v|^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon m_{i}|u_{i}||v|}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1+\varepsilon\tilde{T})}\right\}.

If the parameter is in

(4.7)

\delta\in\left(0,\frac{1}{1+\delta_{MS}}\right),

then the inequality
\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta>0
holds for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
.
Since
1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}>0
, the exponent part arrives its maximum value at
|v|=\frac{\varepsilon|u_{i}|}{1-(1+\varepsilon\tilde{T})\delta}
, leading to

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon\tilde{T}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{\delta\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2[1-(1+\varepsilon\tilde{T})\delta]}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{\delta\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2[1-(1+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|)\delta]}\right\}.

The upper bound for
M_{i}^{\varepsilon}
is recovered as, for any
1\leq i\leq N
,

(4.8)

M_{i}^{\varepsilon}(v)\leq C_{\delta}^{up}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{up}(\delta)c_{i}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta}\quad\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

with notations

C_{\delta}^{up}=\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}},\quad\mathcal{R}_{i}^{up}(\delta)=(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{\delta m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-(1+|\tilde{T}|)\delta)}\right\}.

Finally, we establish the upper bound for
|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{M}_{i}|
. The subtraction can express as

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{M}_{i}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}\,ds\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta},

with the notations

\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}:=\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v-s\varepsilon u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}\right\},

\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}:=\frac{m_{i}(v-s\varepsilon u_{i})\cdot\varepsilon u_{i}}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}+\frac{m_{i}|v-s\varepsilon u_{i}|^{2}\varepsilon\tilde{T}}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})^{2}}-\frac{3\varepsilon\tilde{T}}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}.

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce the following bounds

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)+\frac{s\varepsilon m_{i}u_{i}\cdot v}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\frac{s^{2}\varepsilon^{2}m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)+\frac{\varepsilon m_{i}|u_{i}||v|}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}\right\},

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}|\leq\varepsilon|u_{i}|\frac{m_{i}(|v|+\varepsilon|u_{i}|)}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|\frac{m_{i}(|v|+\varepsilon|u_{i}|)^{2}}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})^{2}}+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|\frac{3}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}.

Here the term
1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}
is positive for any
s\in[0,1]
and for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, as we can assume
\tilde{T}
is small enough.
When the parameter
\delta
satisfies (
4.3
), which guarantees

\delta(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})<1
,
we can distinguish the velocity space into two parts. For
|v|\geq\frac{4\varepsilon|u_{i}|}{1-\delta(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}
, we obtain the upper bounds

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{1}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{1}{1+|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)\right\},

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon|u_{i}|\left(\frac{5}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)\frac{m_{i}|v|}{4}+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|\left(\left(\frac{5}{1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T}}-\delta\right)^{2}\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{32}+\frac{3}{2(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon|u_{i}|\left(\frac{5}{1-|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)\frac{m_{i}|v|}{4}+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|\left(\left(\frac{5}{1-|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)^{2}\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{32}+\frac{3}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)}\right).

For
|v|\leq\frac{4\varepsilon|u_{i}|}{1-\delta(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}
, the exponent part of

\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}
arrives its maximum value at

|v|=\frac{\varepsilon|u_{i}|}{1-\delta(1+s\varepsilon\tilde{T})}
, and we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{m_{i}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)(1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|))}\right\},

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{i}^{s,\varepsilon}|\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon|u_{i}|\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|}{1-|\tilde{T}|}\left(1+\frac{4}{1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|)}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|\left(\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|^{2})}\left(1+\frac{4}{1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|)}\right)^{2}+\frac{3}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)}\right).

These estimates hold for any
s\in[0,1]
, thus the upper bound reads

(4.9)

|\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{M}_{i}|(v)\leq C_{\delta}^{up}(\varepsilon|u_{i}|+\varepsilon|\tilde{T}|)\mathcal{R}_{i}^{sub}(\delta)\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\delta},\quad\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

The notation
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{sub}(\delta)=\max\left\{\mathcal{R}_{i,1}^{sub}(\delta),\mathcal{R}_{i,2}^{sub}(\delta)\right\}
, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{i,1}^{sub}(\delta)=

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{5}{1-|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)\frac{m_{i}|v|}{4}\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{1}{1+|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|}{1-|\tilde{T}|}\left(1+\frac{4}{1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|)}\right)\exp\left\{\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)(1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|))}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{i,2}^{sub}(\delta)

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\left(\frac{5}{1-|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)^{2}\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{32}+\frac{3}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left\{-\frac{m_{i}|v|^{2}}{4}\left(\frac{1}{1+|\tilde{T}|}-\delta\right)\right\}+(\frac{1}{1-|\tilde{T}|})^{\frac{3}{2}}\left[\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|^{2})}\left(1+\frac{4}{1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|)}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{3}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)}\right]\times\exp\left\{\frac{m_{i}|u_{i}|^{2}}{2(1-|\tilde{T}|)(1-\delta(1+|\tilde{T}|))}\right\}.

We end this proof by explaining that
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{low}(\delta),\mathcal{R}_{i}^{up}(\delta)
and

\mathcal{R}_{i}^{sub}(\delta)
can be viewed as positive constants that only depend on
\delta

(note there are different commands for
\delta
). This is because the macroscopic quantities have upper bounds of

\mathcal{O}(\delta_{MS})
, and the parameter
\delta_{MS}
has an upper bound in the smallness assumption (
3.32
).
∎

Based on these estimates for the local Maxwellians
(M_{i}^{\varepsilon})_{1\leq i\leq N}
, we can establish some controls for the operators and the source term arose in the perturbed equation (
2.13
), following the analysis in
[
6
]
. We first focus on the
linear operator
\mathbf{L}^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
2.14
), and split it into two parts, as one usually does for the linearized Boltzmann operator in mono-species case,

\mathbf{L}^{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{K}^{\varepsilon}-\bm{\nu}^{\varepsilon}.

The operator
\mathbf{K}^{\varepsilon}=(\mathit{K}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\dots,\mathit{K}_{N}^{\varepsilon})
is defined by, for any
1\leq i\leq N

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathit{K}_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{g})=\mu_{i}^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathit{B}_{ij}\left(M_{i}^{\varepsilon,\prime}(\mu_{j}^{\prime\ast})^{1/2}g_{j}^{\prime\ast}+M_{j}^{\varepsilon,\prime\ast}(\mu_{i}^{\prime})^{1/2}g_{i}^{\prime}-M_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\mu_{j}^{\ast})^{1/2}g_{j}^{\ast}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{\ast}.

And
